After twenty-three years, the pain of the people of Alevi has not subsided, nor has the fire of the hearts gone out. Well, why? Twenty-three years can take away a lot of pain, but the pain of the Alevi community is still as deep and burning as the first day. Dear lawmakers, we know that unless we face this massacre, not until justice is done, but twenty-three years later, and hundreds of years later, these wounds will not be closed. How did July 2, 1993 happen, and what are the policies that set the stage for this massacre? First of all, I'd like to start by simply reminding you of what happened on July 2, 1993. The 4th edition of the "Pir Sultan Abdal" Festival, organised by Pir Sultan Abdal Association in 1989, was planned between 1 July and 4 July with the participation of intellectuals, artists, writers, poets, journalists, Alevi religious representatives, democratic mass organizations. At the same time, the preparations for the events of Pir Sultan Abdal were still in Sivas, while the event of Alevis was being served as an enemy of Islam through the local press of the city. In the streets of Sivas, give the proper answer. The calls were being made and the Jihad calls were being delivered by the massacrers. In the mosques, announcements were made, and the plan for the massacre was organized step by step. Sivas was brought in from out of the city, organized by more people than ever before. After Friday's prayer on July 2nd, this dark mindset, which systematically organized the massacre, will be a tomb for you with thousands of people. I'm afraid there was no law enforcement or power involved in this group that was moving around in ruins. This group, which later moved towards the convention center where events were being held, continued to attack here. More and more attackers left the convention center and marched into the Madimak Hotel, where intellectuals, artists, writers were staying. According to the governor's report to the Home Secretary after the massacre, the number of attackers increased every hour. The same report says it's 18 p.m. There were 15,000 people in front of the Madimak Hotel that had not been distributed at any time until then. The cars in front of the hotel and the drifting statue were set on fire, and the hotel windows were broken. Meanwhile, government officials were searched at the Madımak Hotel. In their response, they were told, "Don't worry, the necessary measures have been taken. " The footage of the Ihlas News Agency shooting in front of the Madımak Hotel was heard along with slogans of those surrounding the hotel. One called out to the attacker on the first floor of the hotel, "Lan, burn here!," while the other called out, "Hell fire!" Despite the total eight hours, 33 lives were burned alive in flames before the eyes of all mankind. Ladies and gentlemen of the law, what happened in the case of this massacre that organized the forces of darkness behind it, I'd like to talk a little bit about it. According to police records, only 128 defendants were caught in the incidents involving 15,000 people. These defendants have always been protected, unfortunately. Ankara 1 No. In his initial decision, the DGM sentenced the 26 defendants to the charge of manslaughter, according to Article 456, and then reduced them to 15 years with Articles 65/3 of TCK. 37 defendants have been acquitted and 60 defendants have been punished for their violation of the Law on Meetings and Demonstrations. The defendants, convicted of Article 146/3 of the Turkish Penal Code on 19/11/2004 without change, have been released on the grounds that this clause is not in accordance with the new law. The D.A.'s office later requested that the evacuations be withdrawn by saying they were wrong, but the defendants had already escaped. Not a single effort has been made to apprehend the defendants who have been severely punished. Ihsan Erçakmak, who was caught by chance, stated in 1997 that he had served in the court, married in the city of Altıya in 1999, and registered his child in the population, and also worked in an insured business. This person was wanted all over Turkey. One of the most important defendants of the massacre, Jafar Erçakmak, was presented to the court where he resided in France with official documents, but could not be brought in from France. Two years later, this defendant was found dead and buried in the Sivas cemetery. Whether he came to us dead or dead in Sivas, there's still no information, I'm afraid. 15 people have been sought in connection with this case, and although many have been determined by our side as to which countries they reside in, unfortunately, it has not been requested by the authorities. The evaluation form is organised by the head of the committee that investigates the decision. Those who have been issued an evaluation form may request a review of the evaluation form within a month, citing the reasons for the form's registration into the National Judicial Network Information System (UYAP). The request for a re-examine will be unanimous by the agency that conducts a review within a month of the application date. The procedure and basis for the implementation of the above jokes are determined by the regulation imposed by the Supreme Council of Judges and Prosecutors, with the opinion of the Supreme Court and the Court of Appeals. The time appointed for investigation, prosecution or completion of the trial is determined by the Ministry of Justice by the opinion of the Supreme Council of Judges and Prosecutors." tbmm-2016-06-30sit03spe0421par0621	Mr. President, I have made a pledge on behalf of the People's Democratic Party on the 28th Amendment to the State's Law and Some Laws. I salute the General Assembly. This provision envisions the establishment of new special authorities courts and prosecutors. In other words, the special authorities are trying to get into the criminal justice system again. As you know, this arrangement is in violation of Article 37, which regulates the legal rule of the Constitution. Special authorities, on the other hand, have been court-martials that best emulate basic legal principles, such as independence of the judiciary and the neutrality of the judge. Dear lawmakers, the independence of the judiciary and the neutrality of the judiciary in Turkey have been a subject that has been spoken in various regulations for a long time; it may have been one of the things that we talk about most in parliament. Of course, we know very well that there are serious consequences for the political consequences of judicial independence. Mr. President, the principle of judicial independence is defined in the text adopted by the United Nations Human Rights Commission on April 23, 2003, when it entered the literature as the UN Bangalore Judicial Principle of Justice and the basic guarantee of judicial independence is the prerequisite of the legal state and the basic guarantee of fair trial. Therefore, the judge must represent and maintain judicial independence in both individual and institutional aspects. Now, when we look at this bill, which includes arrangements for judges of two high judicial institutions, such as the Supreme Court and the Supreme Court, which we are discussing, it is clear that even basic minimum principles have been ignored. Dear lawmakers, we can also see in various statistics and reports published over the past few years that problems with judicial independence and the neutrality of the judiciary are growing. For example, more than 75 per cent of the European Convention on Human Rights made in violation of Turkey's right to a fair trial between 2012 and 1515. So Turkey is one of the most condemned countries for violating the 6th Amendment. Dear lawmakers, let's look at other institutions. There are contracts and institutions that confirm us, that affect us, that we're committed to, that their reports say the same thing. For example, on April 20, 2015, the Venice Commission filed a report on judicial independence on Turkey. This report states that interference in the judiciary has seriously damaged and violated judicial independence in Turkey, the judge's guarantee. Again, we see the same emphasis in the European Union's 2015 Progress Report, published in November 2015, where the principle of dissolution of powers is said to have been severely violated, and the main reason is that judges and prosecutors are under great political pressure. Dear lawmakers, we are now going through a process in Turkey where the judiciary is quickly politicized, where this politicalisation has reached extremely dangerous levels compared to the past. The politicisation of the judiciary is intended to enable the administration to design the judiciary, appoint judges, appoint specific judges to important cases, and to put pressure on judges individually. The conversion of the judiciary to this extent of intervention is not sustainable either in terms of internal dynamics or the impression of the outside world; it also means that the citizens' minimum sense of confidence in the judiciary will be reset. Dear lawmakers, when it comes to the independence of the judiciary, the basic principles of UN Judiciary Independence of 1985, the recommendation of the 1994 Council of Europe for the Independence of Judges, the Action and Role of the Council of Europe, the 2007 Venice Commission Judiciary Appointments Report and the 2010 Venice Commission's Independentness Report, the international documents that the entire world has been referred to, with the savings involved, are tragic that we, both in our country and in the world's public, are working overtime on arrangements that we may be in a difficult situation. A law state can never be built in a way that defies law. We can't make peace with such arrangements. With these feelings, I salute you all again with respect, with love.
